{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FWPx47GwHoa9RVZtjryddP","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# CanvasChain Symphony 🎼🖼️  \n     2→\n     3→Modular, **artist-friendly** blockchain & NFT platform powered by Rust, gRPC and an event-driven micro-service mesh.\n     4→\n     5→[![CI](https://github.com/your-org/canvaschain/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/your-org/canvaschain/actions/workflows/ci.yml)\n     6→[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-Apache%202.0-blue.svg)](LICENSE)\n     7→\n     8→---\n     9→\n    10→## ✨ Why CanvasChain?\n    11→\n    12→Digital art keeps evolving—so should the chain it lives on. CanvasChain Symphony lets creators compose evolving, multi-layer NFTs that react to collectors, governance votes, DeFi staking and real-world events. Each **movement** of the symphony is a standalone Rust micro-service you can fork, extend or swap out.\n    13→\n    14→* Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI) consensus blends PoS staking with VRF randomness to pick a “composer node.”\n    15→* Factory & Proxy patterns spawn user-defined NFT instruments with upgradeable logic.\n    16→* Event-driven gRPC mesh keeps services decoupled yet reactive.\n    17→* Strategy pattern supports pluggable crypto curves (Ed25519, BLS12-381, SPHINCS+, …).\n    18→* Observer + State Machine render live ownership & trait evolution.\n    19→\n    20→---\n    21→\n    22→## 🌐 High-Level Architecture\n    23→\n    24→```text\n    25→ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n    26→ │                  Proof-of-Inspiration                  │\n    27→ │  (Validator Set + VRF randomness selects Composer)     │\n    28→ └──────┬───────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────┬─┘\n    29→        │               │               │               │\n    30→┌───────▼───────┐ ┌─────▼─────┐   ┌─────▼─────┐   ┌─────▼─────┐\n    31→│ Composition   │ │ Minting   │   │ Remixing  │   │ Marketplace│\n    32→│ Service       │ │ Service   │   │ Service   │   │ Service    │\n    33→└───────────────┘ └───────────┘   └───────────┘   └───────────┘\n    34→        ▲               │               │               ▼\n    35→        │               │               │       ┌──────────────┐\n    36→        └───────────────┴───────────────┴──────▶│ Event Bus    │\n    37→                                                └─────▲────────┘\n    38→                                                      │\n    39→                                             ┌────────┴──────────┐\n    40→                                             │ Wallet Gateway    │\n    41→                                             └───────────────────┘\n    42→```\n    43→\n    44→*RabbitMQ* (or NATS) is the default event bus, but the interface allows Kafka or Redis Streams.\n    45→\n    46→---\n    47→\n    48→## 🛠️ Micro-services\n    49→\n    50→| Service           | Crate | Description                                                   | Port |\n    51→|-------------------|-------|---------------------------------------------------------------|------|\n    52→| `composer-core`   | `composer_core` | PoI consensus, block authoring, VRF beacon             | 7000 |\n    53→| `nft-factory`     | `nft_factory`   | Factory + Proxy pattern for upgradeable NFT contracts  | 7001 |\n    54→| `minting`         | `minting_srv`   | Mints initial NFT layers, IPFS pinning                | 7002 |\n    55→| `remixing`        | `remixing_srv`  | In-place NFT evolution & dynamic trait algorithms     | 7003 |\n    56→| `marketplace`     | `market_srv`    | Order-book & AMM hybrid marketplace                   | 7004 |\n    57→| `royalty-stream`  | `royalty_srv`   | Real-time royalty splits using Superfluid + gStream   | 7005 |\n    58→| `wallet-gateway`  | `wallet_gate`   | gRPC ↔️ JSON-RPC bridge for client wallets             | 7006 |\n    59→| `governance`      | `gov_srv`       | On-chain governance, quadratic voting, CIP upgrades    | 7007 |\n    60→| `defi-orchestra`  | `defi_orch`     | Staking, farming, yield routing for art tokens        | 7008 |\n    61→| `observatory`     | `observer_srv`  | Observer + State Machine for live rendering           | 7009 |\n    62→\n    63→---\n    64→\n    65→## 🚀 Quick Start\n    66→\n    67→### 1. Clone & bootstrap\n    68→\n    69→```bash\n    70→git clone https://github.com/your-org/canvaschain.git\n    71→cd canvaschain\n    72→cargo xtask bootstrap           # installs toolchains & pre-commit hooks\n    73→```\n    74→\n    75→### 2. Start the stack (Docker)\n    76→\n    77→```bash\n    78→docker compose up -d          # starts all 10 services + RabbitMQ + Postgres + IPFS\n    79→```\n    80→\n    81→Logs are streamed using [`cargo-watch`](https://github.com/passcod/cargo-watch):\n    82→\n    83→```bash\n    84→cargo xtask tail composer-core\n    85→```\n    86→\n    87→### 3. Mint your first Canvas NFT\n    88→\n    89→```bash\n    90→curl -X POST \\\n    91→     -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n    92→     -d '{\"artist\":\"0xDEAD…BEEF\",\"layers\":[{\"type\":\"svg\",\"data\":\"<svg>…\"}]}' \\\n    93→     http://localhost:7002/v1/mint\n    94→```\n    95→\n    96→The response includes the NFT CID, token-ID and real-time `ws://…/events` stream URL.\n    97→\n    98→---\n    99→\n   100→## 🧩 Example Rust Client\n   101→\n   102→```rust\n   103→use tonic::transport::Channel;\n   104→use nft_factory::proto::{\n   105→    factory_client::FactoryClient,\n   106→    CreateInstrumentRequest,\n   107→    Curve,\n   108→};\n   109→\n   110→#[tokio::main]\n   111→async fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n   112→    // gRPC client with TLS\n   113→    let mut client = FactoryClient::connect(\"https://localhost:7001\").await?;\n   114→\n   115→    // Request a new upgradeable ERC-721 proxy with Ed25519 curve\n   116→    let req = tonic::Request::new(\n   117→        CreateInstrumentRequest {\n   118→            name:          \"Glitchy Symphony #1\".into(),\n   119→            symbol:        \"GLTCH\".into(),\n   120→            curve:         Curve::Ed25519.into(),\n   121→            royalty_bp:    500,      // 5%\n   122→            max_supply:    10_000,\n   123→            owner_address: \"0xDEAD…BEEF\".into(),\n   124→        }\n   125→    );\n   126→\n   127→    let resp = client.create_instrument(req).await?;\n   128→    println!(\"Instrument deployed at: {}\", resp.get_ref().proxy_address);\n   129→    Ok(())\n   130→}\n   131→```\n   132→\n   133→See `examples/` for more end-to-end flows (mint, remix, list, bid).\n   134→\n   135→---\n   136→\n   137→## ⚙️ Configuration\n   138→\n   139→Environment variables (or `.env`):\n   140→\n   141→```text\n   142→CANVAS_NET_ID=1337\n   143→POSTGRES_URL=postgres://canvas:canvas@db:5432/canvas\n   144→RABBITMQ_URL=amqp://guest:guest@rabbitmq:5672/%2f\n   145→IPFS_API=http://ipfs:5001\n   146→```\n   147→\n   148→Each service also reads its own `[service].toml` for fine-tuning.\n   149→\n   150→---\n   151→\n   152→## 🏗️ Building From Source\n   153→\n   154→```bash\n   155→cargo build --workspace --release\n   156→```\n High-Level Overview\n    11→CanvasChain Symphony is a constellation of **10 Rust micro-services** that collectively operate a modular blockchain-NFT platform for generative and interactive digital art.\n    12→\n    13→* Each “movement” (feature domain) is an **independent service** with a bounded context.  \n    14→* Services communicate via **gRPC** for request/response semantics and a **NATS JetStream** event bus for pub/sub and CQRS projections.  \n    15→* A custom consensus algorithm, **Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)**, blends stake-weighted VRF elections with curator voting to periodically select a “Composer Node” that may commit a generative-art update.\n    16→\n    17→```\n    18→┌──────────────────┐      gRPC        ┌──────────────────┐\n    19→│  Wallet Service  │◀───────────────▶│  Governance Svc  │\n    20→└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n    21→        ▲     ▲                               ▲\n    22→        │     │  Event Bus (NATS JetStream)   │\n    23→        ▼     ▼                               ▼\n    24→┌──────────────────┐      Stream      ┌──────────────────┐\n    25→│  Marketplace Svc │◀───────────────▶│  Minting Svc     │\n    26→└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n    27→```\n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## 2. Micro-service Topology\n    32→\n    33→| Service           | Domain                           | Patterns Used                          |\n    34→|-------------------|----------------------------------|----------------------------------------|\n    35→| `wallet`          | Key management, authentication   | Strategy (crypto curves)               |\n    36→| `governance`      | On-chain DAO voting             | State Machine, Observer                |\n    37→| `minting`         | NFT creation / composition       | Factory, Proxy                         |\n    38→| `remix`           | Trait evolution & layering       | State Machine, Observer                |\n    39→| `marketplace`     | Orderbook & royalties            | Event-Driven, Proxy                    |\n    40→| `staking`         | DeFi incentives / PoI weights    | Strategy, Event-Driven                 |\n    41→| `discovery`       | Metadata & search index          | CQRS, Event Sourcing                   |\n    42→| `composer`        | PoI candidate & block producer   | Strategy, State Machine                |\n    43→| `oracle`          | Off-chain randomness / VRF       | Strategy                               |\n    44→| `monitor`         | Telemetry & health               | Observer                               |\n    45→\n    46→---\n    47→\n    48→## 3. Inter-Service Communication\n    49→\n    50→### 3.1 gRPC Interface Example (Tonic)\n    51→\n    52→```rust\n    53→// crates/minting/src/api.rs\n    54→use tonic::{Request, Response, Status};\n    55→use uuid::Uuid;\n    56→\n    57→tonic::include_proto!(\"canvaschain.minting\");\n    58→\n    59→#[derive(Debug, Default)]\n    60→pub struct MintingApi;\n    61→\n    62→#[tonic::async_trait]\n    63→impl minting_server::Minting for MintingApi {\n    64→    async fn compose_nft(\n    65→        &self,\n    66→        req: Request<ComposeRequest>,\n    67→    ) -> Result<Response<ComposeReply>, Status> {\n    68→        let payload = req.into_inner();\n    69→        let nft_id = Uuid::new_v4();\n    70→\n    71→        // Business logic delegates to domain layer.\n    72→        domain::compose_nft(&payload)\n    73→            .await\n    74→            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?;\n    75→\n    76→        Ok(Response::new(ComposeReply { nft_id: nft_id.to_string() }))\n    77→    }\n    78→}\n    79→```\n    80→\n    81→### 3.2 Event Bus Contract\n    82→\n    83→All domain events are versioned and serialized with **Prost** + **Serde**.  \n    84→Subject naming convention: `canvas.<service>.<aggregate>.<event>`.\n    85→\n    86→```rust\n    87→// events/src/lib.rs\n    88→use chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\n    89→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n    90→\n    91→pub const SCHEMA_VERSION: &str = \"v1\";\n    92→\n    93→#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    94→#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"payload\")]\n    95→pub enum Event {\n    96→    NftComposed(NftComposed),\n    97→    BidPlaced(BidPlaced),\n    98→    GovernanceVoteCast(VoteCast),\n    99→    // ...\n   100→}\n   101→\n   102→#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n   103→pub struct NftComposed {\n   104→    pub nft_id: String,\n   105→    pub composer: String,\n   106→    pub timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,\n   107→    pub layers: Vec<String>,\n   108→}\n   109→```\n   110→\n   111→---\n   112→\n   113→## 4. Core Design Pattern Implementations\n   114→\n   115→### 4.1 Factory Pattern – NFT Instrument Factory\n   116→\n   117→```rust\n   118→// crates/minting/src/factory.rs\n   119→use super::{error::FactoryError, instruments::*};\n   120→\n   121→pub trait InstrumentFactory: Send + Sync {\n   122→    fn spawn(&self, spec: InstrumentSpec) -> Result<Box<dyn Instrument>, FactoryError>;\n   123→}\n   124→\n   125→pub struct DefaultFactory;\n   126→\n   127→impl InstrumentFactory for DefaultFactory {\n   128→    fn spawn(&self, spec: InstrumentSpec) -> Result<Box<dyn Instrument>, FactoryError> {\n   129→        match spec.kind.as_str() {\n   130→            \"Audio\" => Ok(Box::new(AudioInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n   131→            \"Visual\" => Ok(Box::new(VisualInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n   132→            \"Haptic\" => Ok(Box::new(HapticInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n   133→            _ => Err(FactoryError::UnsupportedKind(spec.kind)),\n   134→        }\n   135→    }\n   136→}\n   137→```\n   138→\n   139→### 4.2 Proxy Pattern – Upgradable Smart Contract Proxy\n   140→\n   141→```rust\n   142→// smart_contracts/proxy/src/lib.rs\n   143→use ink::env::call::{build_call, utils::ReturnType};\n   144→use ink::prelude::vec::Vec;\n   145→use ink::primitives::Clear;\n   146→\n   147→#[ink::contract]\n   148→mod proxy {\n   149→    use super::*;\n   150→\n   151→    #[ink(storage)]\n   152→    pub struct Proxy {\n   153→        implementation: AccountId,\n   154→        admin: AccountId,\n   155→    }\n   156→\n   157→    impl Proxy {\n   158→        #[ink(constructor)]\n   159→        pub fn new(implementation: AccountId) -> Self {\n   160→            Self { implementation, admin: Self::env().caller() }\n   161→        }\n   162→\n   163→        #[ink(message)]\n   164→        pub fn upgrade(&mut self, new_impl: AccountId) {\n   165→            assert_eq!(self.env().caller(), self.admin);\n   166→            self.implementation = new_impl;\n   167→        }\n   168→\n   169→        #[ink(message, payable, selector = _)]\n   170→        pub fn fallback(&self) -> Vec<u8> {\n   171→            let result = build_call::<ink::env::DefaultEnvironment>()\n   172→                .call(self.implementation)\n   173→                .transferred_value(Self::env().transferred_value())\n   174→                .exec_input(ink::env::call::ExecutionInput::new(Self::env().selector()).push_arg(Self::env().input()))\n   175→                .returns::<ReturnType<Vec<u8>>>()\n   176→                .fire()\n   177→                .expect(\"proxy call failed\");\n   178→            result\n   179→        }\n   180→    }\n   181→}\n   182→```\n   183→\n   184→### 4.3 Observer Pattern – Trait Evolution Listener\n   185→\n   186→```rust\n   187→// crates/remix/src/observer.rs\n   188→use async_nats::Subscriber;\n   189→use tracing::{debug, error};\n   190→\n   191→pub async fn start_trait_observer(sub: Subscriber) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n   192→    while let Some(msg) = sub.next().await {\n   193→        match serde_json::from_slice::<events::Event>(&msg.payload) {\n   194→            Ok(events::Event::NftComposed(event)) => {\n   195→                debug!(\"Handling NftComposed: {:?}\", event);\n   196→                // Trigger trait evolution workflow.\n   197→            }\n   198→            Ok(_) => {},\n   199→            Err(e) => error!(error = %e, \"Failed to deserialize event\"),\n   200→        }\n   201→    }\n   202→    Ok(())\n   203→}\n   204→```\n   205→\n   206→### 4.4 State Machine – NFT Lifecycle\n   207→\n   208→```rust\n   209→// domain/src/state_machine.rs\n   210→use crate::error::StateError;\n   211→use strum::{Display, EnumIter, EnumString};\n   212→\n   213→#[derive(Clone, Debug, EnumIter, EnumString, Display, PartialEq, Eq)]\n   214→pub enum NftState {\n   215→    Draft,\n   216→    Minted,\n   217→    Listed,\n   218→    Locked,\n   219→    Transferred,\n   220→}\n   221→\n   222→impl NftState {\n   223→    pub fn transit(self, event: &str) -> Result<NftState, StateError> {\n   224→        use NftState::*;\n   225→        Ok(match (self, event) {\n   226→            (Draft, \"mint\") => Minted,\n   227→            (Minted, \"list\") => Listed,\n   228→            (Listed, \"lock\") => Locked,\n   229→            (Locked, \"transfer\") => Transferred,\n   230→            _ => return Err(StateError::InvalidTransition),\n   231→        })\n   232→    }\n   233→}\n   234→```\n   235→\n   236→### 4.5 Strategy Pattern – Pluggable Cryptographic Curves\n   237→\n   238→```rust\n   239→// crates/crypto/src/strategy.rs\n   240→use ed25519_dalek as ed25519;\n   241→use bls_signatures::{PrivateKey as BlsPriv, Serialize as _};\n   242→use pqcrypto_dilithium::dilithium2;\n   243→\n   244→pub trait SigningStrategy: Send + Sync {\n   245→    fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>;\n   246→    fn verify(&self, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool;\n   247→}\n   248→\n   249→pub struct Ed25519Strategy(ed25519::Keypair);\n   250→pub struct BlsStrategy(BlsPriv);\n   251→pub struct DilithiumStrategy(dilithium2::keypair::PublicKey, dilithium2::keypair::SecretKey);\n   252→\n   253→impl SigningStrategy for Ed25519Strategy {\n   254→    fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {\n   255→        self.0.sign(msg).to_bytes().to_vec()\n   256→    }\n   257→    fn verify(&self, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool {\n   258→        ed25519::Signature::from_bytes(sig)\n   259→            .and_then(|s| self.0.verify(msg, &s).map(|_| ()))\n   260→            .is_ok()\n   261→    }\n   262→}\n   263→\n   264→// Implementations for BlsStrategy and DilithiumStrategy omitted for brevity.\n   265→```\n   266→\n   267→---\n   268→\n   269→## 5. Consensus – Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)\n   270→\n   271→```rust\n   272→// consensus/src/poi.rs\n   273→use rand_core::OsRng;\n   274→use vrf::{openssl::CipherSuite, VRF};\n   275→\n   276→pub struct Candidate {\n   277→    pub node_id: String,\n   278→    pub stake: u128,\n   279→    pub curator_score: u64,\n   280→}\n   281→\n   282→pub fn elect_composer(candidates: &[Candidate], epoch: u64) -> Option<String> {\n   283→    let total_weight: f64 = candidates\n   284→        .iter()\n   285→        .map(|c| (c.stake as f64) * 0.8 + (c.curator_score as f64) * 0.2)\n   286→        .sum();\n   287→\n   288→    let vrf_seed = derive_epoch_seed(epoch);\n   289→    let mut highest = None;\n   290→    let mut highest_score = 0f64;\n   291→\n   292→    for c in candidates {\n   293→        // VRF output ∈ [0,1)\n   294→        let vrf_out = vrf_draw(&c.node_id, vrf_seed);\n   295→        let weighted = vrf_out * ((c.stake as f64) + 1.0).ln();\n   296→\n   297→        let final_score = weighted / total_weight;\n   298→        if final_score > highest_score {\n   299→            highest_score = final_score;\n   300→            highest = Some(c.node_id.clone());\n   301→        }\n   302→    }\n   303→    highest\n   304→}\n   305→\n   306→fn vrf_draw(node_id: &str, seed: [u8; 32]) -> f64 {\n   307→    let mut csprng = OsRng;\n   308→    let key = CipherSuite::generate_keypair(&mut csprng);\n   309→    let vrf = key\n   310→        .0\n   311→        .prove(&seed)\n   312→        .expect(\"VRF prove failed\")\n   313→        .hash::<sha2::Sha256>();\n   314→    let num = u128::from_le_bytes(vrf[0..16].try_into().unwrap());\n   315→    (num as f64) / (u128::MAX as f64)\n   316→}\n   317→\n   318→fn derive_epoch_seed(epoch: u64) -> [u8; 32] {\n   319→    use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n   320→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n   321→    hasher.update(epoch.to_le_bytes());\n   322→    hasher.finalize().into()\n   323→}\n   324→```\n   325→\n   326→---\n   327→\n   328→## 6. Error Handling & Observability\n   329→\n   330→* `thiserror` for domain-level error enums.  \n   331→* `anyhow` at service boundaries for rich context.  \n   332→* `tracing` with OpenTelemetry exporters for logs, metrics and spans.  \n   333→* Circuit-breaker middleware on all gRPC clients using `tower::limit::ConcurrencyLimitFuture`.\n   334→\n   335→---\n   336→\n   337→## 7. Security Considerations\n   338→\n   339→1. Hardware-backed key storage (`yubihsm` feature flag).  \n   340→2. Merkle-proof inclusion for off-chain metadata.  \n   341→3. Re-entrancy guards on Ink! contracts via `non_reentrant` macro.  \n   342→4. Formal verification pipeline (K-framework specs in `/formal_specs`).\n   343→\n   344→---\n   345→\n   346→## 8. Deployment Topology (Mermaid)\n   347→\n   348→```mermaid\n   349→graph TD\n   350→  A[Users / DApps] -- gRPC --> B[Ingress (Envoy)]\n   351→  B -- gRPC --> C[Wallet Svc]\n   352→  B -- gRPC --> D[Marketplace Svc]\n   353→  B -- gRPC --> E[Governance Svc]\n   354→  subgraph Stateful\n   355→    F[PostgreSQL]\n   356→    G[Pinot Analytics]\n   357→  end\n   358→  C -- SQL --> F\n   359→  D -- SQL --> F\n   360→  E -- SQL --> F\n   361→  D -- Stream --> G\n   362→  subgraph Chain\n   363→    H[(Composer Nodes)]\n   364→  end\n   365→  H -- PoI blocks --> D\n   366→```\n   367→\n   368→---\n   369→\n   370→## 9. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01523npt5Xjeujwj3Atgm8Qp","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# 001 – Adopt a Monorepo for Rust Microservices\n     2→\n     3→Status: **Approved**  \n     4→Date: 2024-06-17  \n     5→Tags: `repository`, `dev‐experience`, `ci/cd`, `rust`, `microservices`\n     6→\n     7→---\n     8→\n     9→## Context\n    10→\n    11→CanvasChain Symphony orchestrates ten specialized Rust microservices—`composition`, `minting`, `remixing`, `marketplace`, `royalty`, `wallet`, `governance`, `token-management`, `defi`, and `orchestrator`.  \n    12→Early prototypes lived in independent Git repositories. While this respected bounded context, it created friction:\n    13→\n    14→* **Version skew**: cross-service protocol buffers, domain types and cryptographic primitives (e.g., our pluggable `CurveStrategy`) drifted, forcing tedious cherry-picks.\n    15→* **Atomic evolution**: a change to a shared crate (e.g., `canvas-chain-primitives`) required synchronized releases across repos; partial updates risked network halts.\n    16→* **CI💰**: each repo ran its own CI minutes; caching was duplicated and expensive.\n    17→* **Dev onboarding**: cloning ten repos, wiring SSH deploy keys and remembering divergent release processes slowed contributors.\n    18→\n    19→Rust’s native support for [Cargo workspaces](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch14-03-cargo-workspaces.html) and our need for atomic, protocol-grade versioning make a monorepo attractive.\n    20→\n    21→## Decision\n    22→\n    23→We will:\n    24→\n    25→1. Move all Rust microservices, shared crates (`*-primitives`, `*-proto`, etc.) and infrastructure scripts into a single repository named `canvaschain-symphony`.\n    26→2. Organize code using Cargo workspaces:\n    27→\n    28→```\n    29→canvaschain-symphony/\n    30→├── Cargo.toml            # root workspace manifest\n    31→├── crates/\n    32→│   ├── primitives/\n    33→│   ├── proto/\n    34→│   ├── curve-strategy/\n    35→│   └── …\n    36→└── services/\n    37→    ├── composition/\n    38→    ├── minting/\n    39→    └── …\n    40→```\n    41→\n    42→3. Keep **one** `main` branch protected by mandatory checks. Feature work occurs in PRs with review from at least one other domain owner.\n    43→4. Maintain **semantic versioning per crate** using `cargo-release`. A change to `canvaschain-primitives` bumps only that crate, but CI blocks merging unless all dependants still compile.\n    44→5. Employ a top-level `Makefile.toml` (cargo-make) for common developer tasks (`make db`, `make dev-up`, etc.).\n    45→6. Leverage GitHub Actions’ shared cache: incremental build artifacts (~650 MB) are reused across microservices, cutting CI by ~55 %.\n    46→7. Use code-owners to preserve microservice boundaries:\n    47→\n    48→```\n    49→# CODEOWNERS\n    50→/services/composition/ @art-algo @orchestrator\n    51→/services/marketplace/ @defi-team\n    52→/crates/primitives/ @core-protocol\n    53→```\n    54→\n    55→## Consequences\n    56→\n    57→### Positive\n    58→\n    59→* **Atomic protocol upgrades** across services—e.g., adding a new VRF proof to `ProofOfInspiration` requires one PR, one commit hash.\n    60→* Unified **tooling and linting** (`cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo udeps`, `cargo deny`).\n    61→* **Consistent release pipeline**: `cargo make release` builds Docker images for all changed services with version tags derived from Git metadata.\n    62→* **Simplified local dev**: `make dev-up` spins up all services via `docker-compose`, seeded with testnet state.\n    63→* Easier **refactoring**: `cargo fix --workspace` can propagate changes instantly.\n    64→\n    65→### Negative\n    66→\n    67→* Repo size grows quickly (projected 2 GB in 18 months). We will enforce **Git LFS** for large fixtures (e.g., generative art sample sets).\n    68→* Potential for **cross-team merge conflicts**; mitigated by conventional commits and smaller PR scope.\n    69→* Accidental **hidden coupling**; owners must respect microservice boundaries and avoid reaching into sibling domains.\n    70→\n    71→### Mitigations\n    72→\n    73→* **Bazel remote cache** is on our roadmap for even faster incremental builds.\n    74→* Introduce `cargo hack` to run feature-matrix tests only on changed crates.\n    75→* Quarterly **module sociability reviews** to detect leakage in service boundaries.\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## Cargo Workspace Manifest (excerpt)\n    80→\n    81→```toml\n    82→[workspace]\n    83→members = [\n    84→    \"crates/primitives\",\n    85→    \"crates/proto\",\n    86→    \"crates/curve-strategy\",\n    87→    \"services/composition\",\n    88→    \"services/minting\",\n    89→    \"services/remixing\",\n    90→    \"services/marketplace\",\n    91→    \"services/royalty\",\n    92→    \"services/wallet\",\n    93→    \"services/governance\",\n    94→    \"services/token-management\",\n    95→    \"services/defi\",\n    96→    \"services/orchestrator\",\n    97→]\n    98→\n    99→[workspace.dependencies]\n   100→tokio          = { version = \"1.37\", features = [\"rt-multi-thread\", \"macros\"] }\n   101→prost          = \"0.12\"\n   102→tonic          = { version = \"0.11\", features = [\"transport\"] }\n   103→tracing        = \"0.1\"\n   104→anyhow         = \"1\"\n   105→thiserror      = \"1\"\n   106→serde          = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n   107→async-trait    = \"0.1\"\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→---\n   111→\n   112→## Adoption Plan\n   113→\n   114→1. Freeze external repos; merge them into the new `canvaschain-symphony` monorepo preserving commit history (`git subtree add`).\n   115→2. Update CI to run workspace-aware commands (`cargo test --workspace`).\n   116→3. Notify the community via Discord, update contributor docs, schedule “dev-env day” to migrate local clones.\n   117→4. Archive old repositories as read-only.\n   118→\n   119→---\n   120→\n   121→## References\n   122→\n   123→* RFC-0396 – “Cargo Workspaces in Large Rust Projects”  \n   124→* ADR-004 – “Event Bus over gRPC”  \n   125→* Google SRE Book, Chap. 5 – “Monorepos at Scale”\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EyM3fmiag2nHJ2a6DKct9B","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# ADR 002: gRPC for Service-to-Service RPC and NATS Event Bus for Asynchronous Messaging\n     2→\n     3→Date: 2023-11-12  \n     4→Status: Accepted  \n     5→Supersedes: —  \n     6→Superseded by: —  \n     7→Authors: Core Architecture Team (CanvasChain Symphony)\n     8→\n     9→---\n    10→\n    11→## 1. Context\n    12→\n    13→CanvasChain Symphony is composed of ten autonomous Rust micro-services—`composer`, `minter`, `remixer`, `market-maker`, `royalty-streamer`, etc. Each service is owned and deployed independently yet must inter-operate to deliver end-to-end user flows. Two communication patterns are required:\n    14→\n    15→1. **Request / Response (RPC)**  \n    16→   Example: the `market-maker` service must synchronously query `token-registry` for the current royalty split before quoting a swap.\n    17→\n    18→2. **Publish / Subscribe (Eventing)**  \n    19→   Example: the `governance` service votes on a “movement” that triggers the `composer` to mint the next artwork. Every interested service (`gallery-renderer`, `royalty-streamer`, `analytics`) must react to the same chain event without tight coupling.\n    20→\n    21→Key non-functional drivers:\n    22→\n    23→* **Performance:** high throughput, low latency, binary payloads (art layers can be large).\n    24→* **Language Inter-op:** we anticipate plug-ins in Python (ML models), WASM (browser previews), and potentially Solidity-compiled bindings.\n    25→* **Back-Pressure & Delivery Guarantees:** at-least-once delivery for critical NFT state transitions, but fire-and-forget for analytics streams.\n    26→* **Security:** end-to-end encryption (mTLS), pluggable authN/authZ compatible with our wallet integration.\n    27→\n    28→---\n    29→\n    30→## 2. Decision\n    31→\n    32→1. **gRPC** will be adopted for all synchronous, intra-cluster RPC calls.  \n    33→2. **NATS JetStream** will be adopted as the event bus for asynchronous, pub/sub communication.\n    34→\n    35→---\n    36→\n    37→## 3. Rationale\n    38→\n    39→### Why gRPC?\n    40→\n    41→* **IDL-First Development.** Protocol Buffers give us a single source of truth for message schemas in Rust, Python, TypeScript, etc.  \n    42→* **Streaming Support.** Bidirectional streams map naturally to live trait evolution and real-time auction bidding.  \n    43→* **Excellent Rust Ecosystem.** `tonic` provides async/await support, tower middlewares, and first-class mTLS.  \n    44→* **Efficient Binary Encoding.** Artwork layers are sent as deltas or binary blobs; Protobuf minimizes overhead compared to JSON.  \n    45→\n    46→### Why NATS JetStream?\n    47→\n    48→* **Lightweight & Cloud-Native.** Simple single-binary, horizontal-scaling suitability for micro-k8s clusters.  \n    49→* **Exactly-Once Semantics (Opt-In).** JetStream can persist critical events (e.g., ownership transfers) while still allowing fire-and-forget subjects for telemetry.  \n    50→* **Observable.** Built-in tracing, ad-hocs, and integration with Grafana Promtail for distributed tracing.  \n    51→* **Secure Subjects.** Supports leaf-nodes and account isolation, enabling multi-tenant “artist pools.”  \n    52→* **Rust Client Quality.** `nats.rs` offers async, typed-structured messages and JetStream KV for on-chain state mirroring.\n    53→\n    54→### Alternatives Considered\n    55→\n    56→| Option                    | Pros                                              | Cons                                                      |\n    57→|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|\n    58→| REST/JSON over HTTP/2     | Ubiquitous, easy to debug                         | Verbose payloads, no streaming, manual schema evolution   |\n    59→| GraphQL Federation        | Flexible querying                                 | Overkill for service-to-service calls, N + 1 traps        |\n    60→| Apache Kafka              | Durable, massive throughput                       | Operationally heavy, zookeeper (or KRaft) complexity      |\n    61→| RabbitMQ                  | Mature, routing patterns                          | Lower raw throughput, heavier client libs for WASM        |\n    62→| ZeroMQ                    | Embedded-style speed                              | No central broker, discovery challenges                   |\n    63→\n    64→gRPC + NATS best satisfied the combined performance, ergonomics, and operational simplicity we need.\n    65→\n    66→---\n    67→\n    68→## 4. Detailed Design\n    69→\n    70→### 4.1 gRPC Service Definitions\n    71→\n    72→Service contracts live in `proto/` and are versioned via Git tags. An excerpt:\n    73→\n    74→```protobuf\n    75→syntax = \"proto3\";\n    76→\n    77→package canvaschain.market.v1;\n    78→\n    79→service RoyaltyService {\n    80→  // Returns the royalty split for a given NFT\n    81→  rpc GetRoyaltyInfo(GetRoyaltyInfoRequest) returns (GetRoyaltyInfoResponse) {\n    82→    option (google.api.http) = {\n    83→      get: \"/v1/royalty/{token_id}\"\n    84→    };\n    85→  }\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→message GetRoyaltyInfoRequest {\n    89→  uint64 token_id = 1;\n    90→}\n    91→\n    92→message GetRoyaltyInfoResponse {\n    93→  repeated Split split = 1;\n    94→}\n    95→\n    96→message Split {\n    97→  string recipient = 1;         // wallet address\n    98→  uint32 share_bps   = 2;       // basis points\n    99→}\n   100→```\n   101→\n   102→Rust code is generated with `prost` + `tonic_build`:\n   103→\n   104→```rust\n   105→// build.rs\n   106→fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n   107→    tonic_build::configure()\n   108→        .build_server(true)\n   109→        .build_client(true)\n   110→        .out_dir(\"src/proto\")\n   111→        .compile(&[\n   112→            \"proto/canvaschain/market/v1/royalty_service.proto\",\n   113→        ], &[\"proto\"])?;\n   114→    Ok(())\n   115→}\n   116→```\n   117→\n   118→### 4.2 gRPC Infrastructure Patterns\n   119→\n   120→* **Interceptors:** Uniform wallet-signature validation (`x-canvas-sig`), request tracing (OpenTelemetry).  \n   121→* **Resilience:** `tower::retry::Retry` with exponential backoff for transient failures.  \n   122→* **Codegen Regeneration:** GitHub Action enforces that `cargo build --workspace` fails if `proto/` drift is detected.\n   123→\n   124→### 4.3 NATS JetStream Topology\n   125→\n   126→Subject naming convention: `domain.context.version.resource.event`  \n   127→Example: `nft.composer.v1.artwork.minted`\n   128→\n   129→* **Streams:**  \n   130→  * `core.events` – all critical state changes, storage TTL = 365 d, ack = explicit  \n   131→  * `telemetry.metrics` – non-critical stats, storage TTL = 1 d, ack = none  \n   132→* **Consumers:**  \n   133→  * Durable pull consumer per microservice (`composer_core`, `gallery_renderer`, …).  \n   134→  * Ephemeral consumers for CLI tools and data scientists.\n   135→\n   136→Rust subscription example:\n   137→\n   138→```rust\n   139→let event: MintedEvent = jetstream\n   140→    .get_stream(\"core.events\")?\n   141→    .create_consumer(ConsumerConfig {\n   142→        durable_name: Some(\"gallery_renderer\".to_string()),\n   143→        filter_subject: \"nft.composer.v1.artwork.minted\".into(),\n   144→        deliver_policy: DeliverPolicy::All,\n   145→        ack_policy: AckPolicy::Explicit,\n   146→        ..Default::default()\n   147→    })?\n   148→    .messages()?\n   149→    .next()\n   150→    .await?\n   151→    .unwrap()\n   152→    .deserialize()?;\n   153→```\n   154→\n   155→### 4.4 Transactional Outbox Pattern\n   156→\n   157→To avoid dual-write hazards between the PostgreSQL command side and the event bus, each service implements the **Transactional Outbox** pattern. A reliable background task (`tokio::spawn`) pulls unsent events, publishes to NATS, and marks them dispatched. This guarantees at-least-once delivery even if the service crashes mid-transaction.\n   158→\n   159→![Transactional Outbox Diagram](../../design/diagrams/transactional_outbox.png)\n   160→\n   161→---\n   162→\n   163→## 5. Security Considerations\n   164→\n   165→* **mTLS Everywhere:** Shared SPIFFE IDs (`spiffe://canvaschain/{service}`) issued by cert-manager.  \n   166→* **Subject ACLs:** Only the `composer` may publish on `nft.composer.*`; others read-only.  \n   167→* **JWT Claims:** gRPC headers leverage short-lived JWTs signed by the `auth` service, propagated via NATS headers for traceability.  \n   168→* **Replay Protection:** Nonces + timestamp fields in all state-changing Protobuf messages.\n   169→\n   170→---\n   171→\n   172→## 6. Consequences\n   173→\n   174→### Positive\n   175→\n   176→* Consistent developer experience across sync and async messaging.\n   177→* Performance-optimized binary encoding keeps latency < 15 ms P99 inside the same AZ.\n   178→* Pluggable structure: any service can be rewritten in another language with minimal friction.\n   179→* Observability is unified via OpenTelemetry exporters for `tonic` and `nats.rs`.\n   180→\n   181→### Negative / Trade-offs\n   182→\n   183→* Learning curve for contributors unfamiliar with Protocol Buffers.\n   184→* Operationally, we must maintain a NATS cluster (RAFT group) in addition to the Postgres DB.\n   185→* Protobuf evolution requires backward-compatibility discipline to avoid breaking older miners.\n   186→\n   187→---\n   188→\n   189→## 7. Measures of Success\n   190→\n   191→1. End-to-end mint-to-marketplace latency ≤ 200 ms on staging.\n   192→2. Zero lost critical events during chaos-testing (`chaos-nats` network partitions).\n   193→3. Successful inter-op PoC of Python `remixer` plugin subscribing to `nft.*` events.\n   194→\n   195→---\n   196→\n   197→## 8. References\n   198→\n   199→* NATS JetStream Design: https://docs.nats.io/jetstream  \n   200→* tonic gRPC Rust Library: https://github.com/hyperium/tonic  \n   201→* “Transactional Outbox” by Pat Helland: https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html  \n   202→* gRPC Security Best Practices: https://grpc.io/docs/guides/security/\n   203→\n   204→---\n   205→\n   206→*(END OF ADR)*\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Qqf6Cx3jbtAFTWuQRFS2qN","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# Architecture Decision Record 003  \n     3→**Pluggable Cryptography Strategy**\n     4→\n     5→| Status | Proposed |\n     6→| ------ | -------- |\n     7→| Date   | 2024-05-05 |\n     8→| Target release | `v0.8.0` (`CanvasChain Symphony`) |\n     9→| Supersedes | — |\n    10→| Related ADRs | ADR-001 Modular Micro-services, ADR-002 Event Bus |\n    11→\n    12→---\n    13→\n    14→## 1. Context\n    15→\n    16→CanvasChain Symphony aims to be the de-facto playground for experimental digital art on the blockchain.  \n    17→Consequently, we must:\n    18→\n    19→1. Support *multiple* signature schemes—`Ed25519` for mainstream wallets, `BLS12-381` for aggregated votes, and one or more post-quantum candidates (e.g. `Dilithium`, `Falcon`) for future-proofing.\n    20→2. Allow *runtime* selection of a scheme for each micro-service (e.g. the **governance** service needs BLS while **composition** might stick with Ed25519 for speed).\n    21→3. Offer *hot-swap* capabilities so research collectives can ship cryptographic experiments without forking or recompiling the **entire** chain.\n    22→4. Maintain *deterministic* behavior across WASM smart-contracts and native nodes so that consensus is never broken.\n    23→5. Remain *auditable* and *testable*—adding new crypto must not impair formal verification or fuzzing pipelines.\n    24→\n    25→## 2. Decision\n    26→\n    27→We will implement a **Strategy Pattern** that surfaces an `CryptoSuite` trait across all micro-services.  \n    28→A concrete suite is loaded **at startup** according to configuration (environment variable, CLI flag, or chain parameter) and injected via dependency-injection (gRPC metadata and event bus headers propagate the chosen suite).\n    29→\n    30→Key points:\n    31→\n    32→* The `CryptoSuite` trait is **no-std** compatible so that the same code compiles to WASM.\n    33→* Each suite resides in its own crate (`canvas_crypto_ed25519`, `canvas_crypto_bls`, `canvas_crypto_dilithium`, …) behind a *cargo feature*.\n    34→* We provide a *registry* that maps the on-chain enum discriminator (`CryptoAlgoId`) to a boxed trait object.\n    35→* Critical operations (sign, verify, aggregate, random_seed) are **generic** over the trait, avoiding `dyn` in hot paths.\n    36→* The consensus engine restricts the active set of suites to those that produce deterministic results for VRF.  \n    37→  A governance proposal can *enable* or *disable* a suite network-wide.\n    38→\n    39→### High-level module layout\n    40→\n    41→```\n    42→canvaschain/\n    43→ ├─ crates/\n    44→ │   ├─ crypto_api/       # defines trait + registry\n    45→ │   ├─ crypto_ed25519/\n    46→ │   ├─ crypto_bls/\n    47→ │   └─ crypto_dilithium/\n    48→ └─ services/\n    49→     ├─ governance/\n    50→     ├─ composition/\n    51→     └─ …\n    52→```\n    53→\n    54→### Rust Interface (simplified)\n    55→\n    56→```rust\n    57→// crypto_api/src/lib.rs\n    58→#![no_std]\n    59→\n    60→use alloc::boxed::Box;\n    61→use core::fmt::Debug;\n    62→\n    63→pub type Result<T> = core::result::Result<T, CryptoError>;\n    64→\n    65→#[derive(Debug)]\n    66→pub enum CryptoError {\n    67→    InvalidKey,\n    68→    InvalidSignature,\n    69→    VerificationFailed,\n    70→    UnsupportedAlgo,\n    71→}\n    72→\n    73→pub trait CryptoSuite: Send + Sync + Debug + 'static {\n    74→    const ID: CryptoAlgoId;\n    75→\n    76→    fn generate_keypair(&self, rng: &mut dyn RngCore) -> Result<(PublicKey, PrivateKey)>;\n    77→    fn sign(&self, privkey: &PrivateKey, msg: &[u8]) -> Result<Signature>;\n    78→    fn verify(&self, pubkey: &PublicKey, msg: &[u8], sig: &Signature) -> Result<()>;\n    79→\n    80→    // Optional for schemes that support it\n    81→    fn aggregate(&self, sigs: &[Signature]) -> Result<Signature> {\n    82→        Err(CryptoError::UnsupportedAlgo)\n    83→    }\n    84→}\n    85→\n    86→pub enum CryptoAlgoId {\n    87→    Ed25519,\n    88→    Bls12381,\n    89→    Dilithium5,\n    90→    // …\n    91→}\n    92→\n    93→pub struct SuiteRegistry {\n    94→    inner: spin::RwLock<hashbrown::HashMap<CryptoAlgoId, Box<dyn CryptoSuite>>>,\n    95→}\n    96→\n    97→impl SuiteRegistry {\n    98→    pub const fn new() -> Self { /* … */ }\n    99→    pub fn register(&self, suite: Box<dyn CryptoSuite>) { /* … */ }\n   100→    pub fn get(&self, id: CryptoAlgoId) -> Result<&dyn CryptoSuite> { /* … */ }\n   101→}\n   102→```\n   103→\n   104→## 3. Consequences\n   105→\n   106→### Positive\n   107→\n   108→* **Extensibility**: New crypto only requires a new crate implementing `CryptoSuite` and an entry in the registry.\n   109→* **Selective compilation**: Production nodes can compile with `--no-default-features --features \"ed25519 bls\"` to minimize the binary’s attack surface.\n   110→* **WASM parity**: Smart-contracts rely on the same trait, guaranteeing deterministic crypto across FFI boundaries.\n   111→* **Governance control**: The registry honors an on-chain allow-list, so malicious or broken suites can be disabled without a hard-fork.\n   112→\n   113→### Negative / Trade-offs\n   114→\n   115→* **Runtime dispatch** adds minimal overhead (~2–5 ns) when comparing trait object lookup vs. monomorphized code.\n   116→* **Larger codebase**: Each algorithm introduces external dependencies (`blst`, `pqcrypto`). Extra scrutiny is required for audits.\n   117→* **Complex migration** if the active suite for validators changes (requires key rotation mechanics).\n   118→\n   119→## 4. Alternatives considered\n   120→\n   121→1. **Compile-time feature gating only**  \n   122→   Reject because it forbids late activation of new algorithms by governance.\n   123→2. **Dynamic linking to system crypto libraries**  \n   124→   Reject due to WASM determinism concerns and platform-specific quirks.\n   125→3. **One-size-fits-all (ED25519 only)**  \n   126→   Reject; stifles experimentation and future-proofing against quantum attacks.\n   127→\n   128→## 5. Implementation plan\n   129→\n   130→1. Land `crypto_api` crate with trait, error types, and `SuiteRegistry`.  \n   131→2. Port existing ED25519 utilities to `canvas_crypto_ed25519`; register by default.  \n   132→3. Integrate `blst` and expose `canvas_crypto_bls`.  \n   133→4. Scaffold a proof-of-concept `canvas_crypto_dilithium` (behind `experimental` feature).  \n   134→5. Update all micro-services to inject `SuiteRegistry` from their service bootstrap.  \n   135→6. Add CLI flag `--crypto-suite=[ed25519|bls|dilithium]` for node operators.  \n   136→7. Extend governance pallet to manage the on-chain allow-list.  \n   137→8. Write cross-suite property tests and differential fuzzers (via `cargo-fuzz`).  \n   138→9. Perform third-party audit before enabling `dilithium` on mainnet.\n   139→\n   140→## 6. Security considerations\n   141→\n   142→* Using *multiple* cryptographic primitives increases the surface area for supply-chain attacks. Each suite is audited independently.\n   143→* Each suite’s implementation must meet **constant-time** guarantees.  \n   144→  CI integrates `criterion-ct` to detect data-dependent timing.\n   145→* The registry denies loading suites compiled with *unsafe optimizations* (e.g., `-C target-cpu=native`) unless explicitly whitelisted by validators.\n   146→* Key-material memory is zeroized via `zeroize` crate to mitigate side-channel leaks.\n   147→\n   148→## 7. Reference implementation\n   149→\n   150→Refer to `examples/demo_pluggable_crypto.rs` for a runnable showcase.  \n   151→The demo spins up a mock governance vote that swaps from Ed25519 to BLS mid-session and re-verifies historical signatures.\n   152→\n   153→```\n   154→cargo run --example demo_pluggable_crypto --features \"ed25519 bls\"\n   155→```\n   156→\n   157→---\n   158→\n   159→*Prepared by*: `@rust_blockchain_nft_expert`  \n   160→*Reviewed by*: `core-team, cryptography-guild`  \n   161→*Last updated*: 2024-05-05\n   162→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C3uJLiq1Tf3nvXcve48SFZ","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→<!--\n     3→    File: docs/governance_protocol.md\n     4→    Project: CanvasChain Symphony – rust_blockchain_nft_expert_071\n     5→    --------------------------------------------------------------\n     6→    This document is the single source-of-truth for the on-chain\n     7→    governance mechanism that drives protocol evolution, curator\n     8→    incentives, and generative-art movements across the Symphony.\n     9→    It is intended for core developers, tooling authors, auditors,\n    10→    and NFT collectives who integrate directly with the runtime.\n    11→-->\n    12→\n    13→# CanvasChain Symphony Governance Protocol\n    14→\n    15→CanvasChain Symphony is governed entirely on-chain through a\n    16→token-weighted, stake-weighted process that combines **Proof-of-\n    17→Inspiration (PoI)** with a modular on-chain council and an open\n    18→referenda system.  \n    19→The protocol is implemented in Rust and compiled to WebAssembly\n    20→for deterministic smart-contract execution, while the **Governance\n    21→Microservice** orchestrates off-chain tasks and gRPC messaging.\n    22→\n    23→---\n    24→\n    25→## Table of Contents\n    26→\n    27→1. Goals & Principles\n    28→2. Governing Token: `CNOTE`\n    29→3. Proposal Lifecycle\n    30→4. Voting & Delegation Model\n    31→5. Smart-Contract Interface (Rust/ink!)\n    32→6. Governance gRPC Microservice\n    33→7. Event Bus Topics\n    34→8. Slashing, Rewards & Game-Theory\n    35→9. Upgrade & Migration Path\n    36→10. Security Considerations\n    37→\n    38→---\n    39→\n    40→## 1. Goals & Principles\n    41→\n    42→* **Artist-Centric** – Parameters must empower creators to evolve\n    43→  their work without centralised gatekeepers.\n    44→* **Modularity** – Every rule is a smart contract that can be\n    45→  hot-swapped under strict upgrade procedures.\n    46→* **Transparency** – All governance data is stored on-chain and\n    47→  emitted as signed events to the public event bus.\n    48→* **Safety** – Slashing and veto-timelocks minimise malicious or\n    49→  rushed protocol changes.\n    50→\n    51→---\n    52→\n    53→## 2. Governing Token: `CNOTE` 🎼\n    54→\n    55→Symbol            | `CNOTE`\n    56→------------------|----------------------------\n    57→Type              | ERC-20-compatible substrate asset\n    58→Use-cases         | Voting, staking, fee rebate, slashing collateral\n    59→Inflation Model   | Dynamic, linked to generative movement cadence\n    60→Initial Supply    | `10 000 000.0000 CNOTE`\n    61→\n    62→### 2.1 Staking for PoI\n    63→\n    64→Staked `CNOTE` participates in **Proof-of-Inspiration** to elect the\n    65→next *Composer Node*. During the same epoch, these staking positions\n    66→also determine governance voting power.\n    67→\n    68→---\n    69→\n    70→## 3. Proposal Lifecycle\n    71→\n    72→```\n    73→Draft → Review → Voting → Enactment (↻ or ⛔)\n    74→```\n    75→\n    76→Phase        | Duration | Key Actors                    | Abort €\n    77→-------------|----------|------------------------------|--------\n    78→Draft        | ∞        | Author                       | Free\n    79→Review       | 48 h     | Council (optional)           | ±0.1 C\n    80→Voting       | 72 h     | Token-Holders & Delegates    | Bond\n    81→Enactment    | 24 h     | Runtime Scheduler + VRF      | –\n    82→\n    83→*Abort fee is burned to discourage spam.*\n    84→\n    85→### 3.1 State Machine\n    86→\n    87→The following Rust enum matches the state machine stored on-chain:\n    88→\n    89→```rust\n    90→/// Governance proposal lifecycle.\n    91→#[derive(scale::Encode, scale::Decode, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n    92→pub enum ProposalStage {\n    93→    Draft,\n    94→    Review { started_at: BlockNumber },\n    95→    Voting { ends_at: BlockNumber },\n    96→    Enactment { scheduled_for: BlockNumber },\n    97→    Rejected,\n    98→    Executed,\n    99→}\n   100→```\n   101→\n   102→---\n   103→\n   104→## 4. Voting & Delegation Model\n   105→\n   106→1 `CNOTE` staked = **1 voting unit**.  \n   107→Voters may **delegate** to any on-chain account (wallet, DAO\n   108→multisig, or artist profile). Delegations are *revocable* at any\n   109→time before the snapshot block.\n   110→\n   111→### 4.1 Conviction Escalation\n   112→\n   113→Inspired by Polkadot’s conviction-voting, longer lockups multiply\n   114→weight:\n   115→\n   116→Lockup (blocks) | Multiplier\n   117→---------------|-----------\n   118→0              | ×1\n   119→80 k (≈1 week) | ×2\n   120→320 k          | ×3\n   121→1 280 k        | ×4\n   122→\n   123→---\n   124→\n   125→## 5. Smart-Contract Interface\n   126→\n   127→Below is the canonical ink! interface shipped in the **governance**\n   128→pallet. Forks can use the Factory pattern to spawn customised\n   129→instances.\n   130→\n   131→```rust\n   132→//! ink! smart contract generated by `cargo contract new`.\n   133→#![cfg_attr(not(feature = \"std\"), no_std)]\n   134→\n   135→#[ink::contract]\n   136→mod symphony_governance {\n   137→    use ink::storage::Mapping;\n   138→    use scale::{Decode, Encode};\n   139→\n   140→    /// Unique identifier for on-chain proposals.\n   141→    pub type ProposalId = u64;\n   142→\n   143→    #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Encode, Decode)]\n   144→    #[cfg_attr(feature = \"std\", derive(scale_info::TypeInfo))]\n   145→    pub enum VoteKind {\n   146→        /// Approve the proposal.\n   147→        Aye,\n   148→        /// Reject the proposal.\n   149→        Nay,\n   150→        /// Abstain—counts for quorum, not for tally.\n   151→        Abstain,\n   152→    }\n   153→\n   154→    #[derive(Debug, Encode, Decode)]\n   155→    #[cfg_attr(feature = \"std\", derive(scale_info::TypeInfo))]\n   156→    pub struct Proposal {\n   157→        author: AccountId,\n   158→        ipfs_hash: [u8; 46], // IPFS CIDv1 (base58)\n   159→        stage: ProposalStage,\n   160→        aye: u128,\n   161→        nay: u128,\n   162→        abstain: u128,\n   163→        vetoed: bool,\n   164→    }\n   165→\n   166→    #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Encode, Decode)]\n   167→    #[cfg_attr(feature = \"std\", derive(scale_info::TypeInfo))]\n   168→    pub enum ProposalStage {\n   169→        Draft,\n   170→        Review,\n   171→        Voting,\n   172→        Enactment,\n   173→        Executed,\n   174→        Rejected,\n   175→    }\n   176→\n   177→    #[ink(storage)]\n   178→    pub struct Governance {\n   179→        proposals: Mapping<ProposalId, Proposal>,\n   180→        next_id: ProposalId,\n   181→        council: Vec<AccountId>,\n   182→    }\n   183→\n   184→    impl Governance {\n   185→        #[ink(constructor)]\n   186→        pub fn new(council: Vec<AccountId>) -> Self {\n   187→            Self {\n   188→                proposals: Mapping::default(),\n   189→                next_id: 0,\n   190→                council,\n   191→            }\n   192→        }\n   193→\n   194→        /// Submit an initial draft. Requires a small bond.\n   195→        #[ink(message, payable)]\n   196→        pub fn submit_proposal(&mut self, ipfs_hash: [u8; 46]) -> ProposalId {\n   197→            // Bond check\n   198→            assert!(\n   199→                self.env().transferred_balance() >= 10_000_000_000, // 0.01 CNOTE\n   200→                \"Insufficient bond\"\n   201→            );\n   202→\n   203→            let author = self.env().caller();\n   204→            let id = self.next_id;\n   205→            self.next_id += 1;\n   206→\n   207→            let proposal = Proposal {\n   208→                author,\n   209→                ipfs_hash,\n   210→                stage: ProposalStage::Draft,\n   211→                aye: 0,\n   212→                nay: 0,\n   213→                abstain: 0,\n   214→                vetoed: false,\n   215→            };\n   216→\n   217→            self.proposals.insert(id, &proposal);\n   218→            self.env().emit_event(NewProposal { id, author });\n   219→            id\n   220→        }\n   221→\n   222→        /// Cast a vote with conviction weight.\n   223→        #[ink(message)]\n   224→        pub fn vote(\n   225→            &mut self,\n   226→            id: ProposalId,\n   227→            kind: VoteKind,\n   228→            weight: u128,\n   229→        ) -> Result<(), GovernanceError> {\n   230→            let mut p = self\n   231→                .proposals\n   232→                .get(id)\n   233→                .ok_or(GovernanceError::ProposalMissing)?;\n   234→\n   235→            ensure!(\n   236→                p.stage == ProposalStage::Voting,\n   237→                GovernanceError::NotInVoting\n   238→            );\n   239→\n   240→            match kind {\n   241→                VoteKind::Aye => p.aye += weight,\n   242→                VoteKind::Nay => p.nay += weight,\n   243→                VoteKind::Abstain => p.abstain += weight,\n   244→            }\n   245→\n   246→            self.proposals.insert(id, &p);\n   247→            self.env().emit_event(VoteCast {\n   248→                id,\n   249→                voter: self.env().caller(),\n   250→                kind,\n   251→                weight,\n   252→            });\n   253→            Ok(())\n   254→        }\n   255→\n   256→        // Additional methods: advance_stage, veto, execute, etc.\n   257→    }\n   258→\n   259→    /// EVENTS -----------------------------------------------------\n   260→    #[ink(event)]\n   261→    pub struct NewProposal {\n   262→        #[ink(topic)]\n   263→        id: ProposalId,\n   264→        #[ink(topic)]\n   265→        author: AccountId,\n   266→    }\n   267→\n   268→    #[ink(event)]\n   269→    pub struct VoteCast {\n   270→        #[ink(topic)]\n   271→        id: ProposalId,\n   272→        #[ink(topic)]\n   273→        voter: AccountId,\n   274→        kind: VoteKind,\n   275→        weight: u128,\n   276→    }\n   277→\n   278→    /// ERRORS -----------------------------------------------------\n   279→    #[derive(Debug, Encode, Decode)]\n   280→    #[cfg_attr(feature = \"std\", derive(scale_info::TypeInfo))]\n   281→    pub enum GovernanceError {\n   282→        ProposalMissing,\n   283→        NotInVoting,\n   284→        Unauthorized,\n   285→    }\n   286→\n   287→    fn ensure(cond: bool, err: GovernanceError) -> Result<(), GovernanceError> {\n   288→        if cond {\n   289→            Ok(())\n   290→        } else {\n   291→            Err(err)\n   292→        }\n   293→    }\n   294→}\n   295→```\n   296→\n   297→---\n   298→\n   299→## 6. Governance gRPC Microservice\n   300→\n   301→The **`gov-orchestrator`** microservice bridges off-chain tools, the\n   302→event bus, and on-chain ink! contracts.\n   303→\n   304→### 6.1 Proto Definition (`proto/gov.proto`)\n   305→\n   306→```proto\n   307→syntax = \"proto3\";\n   308→\n   309→package symphony.gov;\n   310→\n   311→service Governance {\n   312→  rpc SubmitProposal (SubmitProposalRequest) returns (ProposalReply);\n   313→  rpc CastVote       (CastVoteRequest)       returns (VoteReply);\n   314→  rpc Subscribe      (SubscribeRequest)      returns (stream GovEvent);\n   315→}\n   316→\n   317→message SubmitProposalRequest {\n   318→  bytes ipfs_cid = 1;\n   319→  uint64 bond    = 2;\n   320→}\n   321→\n   322→message CastVoteRequest {\n   323→  uint64 proposal_id = 1;\n   324→  VoteKind kind      = 2;\n   325→  uint128 weight     = 3;\n   326→}\n   327→\n   328→enum VoteKind {\n   329→  AYE      = 0;\n   330→  NAY      = 1;\n   331→  ABSTAIN  = 2;\n   332→}\n   333→\n   334→message SubscribeRequest {}\n   335→\n   336→message GovEvent {\n   337→  oneof event {\n   338→    NewProposal new_proposal = 1;\n   339→    VoteCast    vote_cast    = 2;\n   340→  }\n   341→}\n   342→\n   343→message ProposalReply { uint64 proposal_id = 1; }\n   344→message VoteReply     { bool   accepted     = 1; }\n   345→```\n   346→\n   347→### 6.2 Tonic Implementation (excerpt)\n   348→\n   349→```rust\n   350→pub struct GovernanceService {\n   351→    contract: ink_wrapper::GovernanceClient,\n   352→    event_tx: broadcast::Sender<GovEvent>,\n   353→}\n   354→\n   355→#[tonic::async_trait]\n   356→impl Governance for GovernanceService {\n   357→    async fn submit_proposal(\n   358→        &self,\n   359→        req: Request<SubmitProposalRequest>,\n   360→    ) -> Result<Response<ProposalReply>, Status> {\n   361→        let caller = extract_signer(&req)?;\n   362→        let id = self\n   363→            .contract\n   364→            .with(caller)\n   365→            .submit_proposal(req.get_ref().ipfs_cid, req.get_ref().bond)\n   366→            .await\n   367→            .map_err(map_contract_err)?;\n   368→        Ok(Response::new(ProposalReply { proposal_id: id }))\n   369→    }\n   370→\n   371→    // … other RPC handlers …\n   372→}\n   373→```\n   374→\n   375→---\n   376→\n   377→## 7. Event Bus Topics\n   378→\n   379→Topic                 | Payload Struct             | Source\n   380→----------------------|----------------------------|--------\n   381→`gov.proposal.new`    | `NewProposal`              | ink! contract\n   382→`gov.vote.cast`       | `VoteCast`                 | ink! contract\n   383→`gov.stage.changed`   | `StageChanged`             | orchestrator\n   384→`gov.composer.elected`| `ComposerElected(Addr)`    | consensus\n   385→\n   386→All events are signed with the node’s Ed25519 key and published via\n   387→NATS.\n   388→\n   389→---\n   390→\n   391→## 8. Slashing, Rewards & Game-Theory\n   392→\n   393→• **Spam Slashing** – Malformed or empty proposals forfeit their\n   394→bond.  \n   395→• **Composer Misconduct** – Composer nodes that fail to publish an\n   396→assigned movement within the VRF epoch lose 5 % of stake.  \n   397→• **Participation Rewards** – 0.5 % of epoch inflation is\n   398→distributed pro-rata to addresses that voted on >=75 % of proposals\n   399→during the epoch.\n   400→\n   401→Mathematical model: see `/research/slashing_model.ipynb`.\n   402→\n   403→---\n   404→\n   405→## 9. Upgrade & Migration Path\n   406→\n   407→1. `Upgrade` proposal passes with 2/3 super-majority.  \n   408→2. New runtime WASM is stored on-chain (`set_code`).  \n   409→3. 24-hour timelock triggers.  \n   410→4. All microservices auto-download the new ABI via gRPC call\n   411→   `GetRuntimeMeta`.  \n   412→5. If ≥30 % of stake sends `veto_upgrade`, the scheduler aborts.\n   413→\n   414→---\n   415→\n   416→## 10. Security Considerations\n   417→\n   418→* Re-entrancy guarded by ink! `#[modifier(non_reentrant)]`.\n   419→* All cross-contract calls use **Weight Metering** to prevent DoS.\n   420→* gRPC endpoints enforce **mTLS** with per-microservice certificates.\n   421→* Formal verification of the governance contract with `cargo-prove`\n   422→  (K-framework).\n   423→\n   424→---\n   425→\n   426→### Appendix A – CLI Usage\n   427→\n   428→```bash\n   429→# Submit a proposal (assumes you’re on the Polkadot.js CLI)\n   430→symphony-cli gov submit \\\n   431→  --ipfs QmQz... \\\n   432→  --bond 0.01\n   433→\n   434→# Cast an AYE vote with ×2 conviction\n   435→symphony-cli gov vote 42 --aye --lock 1w\n   436→\n   437→# Follow governance events\n   438→symphony-cli events tail gov.*\n   439→```\n   440→\n   441→---\n   442→\n   443→_© 2024 CanvasChain Symphony Core. MIT-Apache-2.0 licensed._\n   444→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ni3c2UVvqmNBF8vQ6ZCBrE","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# CanvasChain Symphony — NFT Evolution Specification\n     3→*Version: 1.0.0 | Status: Draft | Last-Updated: 2024-05-18*\n     4→\n     5→> “Every artwork is a living organism.  \n     6→>  — *V. Kandinsky (probably)”*\n     7→\n     8→---\n     9→\n    10→## 1 ‑ Purpose\n    11→\n    12→The goal of this document is to **formally describe the life-cycle and on-chain\n    13→state machine** that governs a _Multilayer NFT_ (mNFT) inside CanvasChain\n    14→Symphony.  \n    15→It serves as:\n    16→\n    17→1. A contract between backend engineers, smart-contract authors, frontend\n    18→   clients, and governance maintainers.\n    19→2. A reference for implementers of **`canvaschain-nft-runtime`** (Rust micro-service),\n    20→   **gRPC APIs**, and **Protocol Buffers**.\n    21→3. A knowledge-base for artists and DAO voters to understand how their decisions\n    22→   move an artwork forward.\n    23→\n    24→---\n    25→\n    26→## 2 ‑ High-Level Overview\n    27→\n    28→```\n    29→+-------------------+          +-------------------+\n    30→|   Composer Node   |  emits   |    Event Bus      |\n    31→| (PoI Consensus)   +--------->+ (NATS JetStream)  |\n    32→+-------------------+          +----------+--------+\n    33→                                         |\n    34→                                         |   event::NftEvolved\n    35→                                         v\n    36→+-------------------+      gRPC   +------+------+\n    37→|  NFT Runtime Svc  +------------>+  State DB   |\n    38→|  (Rust / tonic)   |  state      | (sled /   ) |\n    39→+--------+----------+             | Postgres ) |\n    40→         |                        +------------+\n    41→         | Observes\n    42→         v\n    43→+-------------------+            +----------------+\n    44→|    Observer UI    |  websockets|   Public API   |\n    45→+-------------------+            +----------------+\n    46→```\n    47→\n    48→1. **Composer Node** — Selected via *Proof-of-Inspiration* (PoI) to publish\n    49→   generative updates.\n    50→2. **Event Bus** — Decouples micro-services (NATS).\n    51→3. **NFT Runtime Svc** — Maintains canonical state machine + verification.\n    52→4. **Observer UI** — Real-time ownership & trait visualization.\n    53→\n    54→---\n    55→\n    56→## 3 ‑ NFT State Machine\n    57→\n    58→```\n    59→stateDiagram-v2\n    60→    [*] --> Seeded : Minted\n    61→    Seeded --> Dormant : Freeze\n    62→    Dormant --> Flourishing : Inspire\n    63→    Flourishing --> Flourishing : Evolve*\n    64→    Flourishing --> Fragmented : Split\n    65→    Fragmented --> Dormant : Merge\n    66→    Flourishing --> Archived : Burn\n    67→    Dormant --> Archived : Burn\n    68→    Archived --> [*]\n    69→\n    70→    note right of Seeded : Layer-0 metadata stored\n    71→    note left of Flourishing : Layer-N traits evolve\n    72→```\n    73→\n    74→### 3.1 ‑ State Definitions\n    75→\n    76→| State        | Description                                                                               |\n    77→|--------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n    78→| `Seeded`     | Newly minted. Contains only base DNA and creator signature.                               |\n    79→| `Dormant`    | Frozen to preserve a snapshot. Cannot evolve until *Inspire*.                             |\n    80→| `Flourishing`| Fully mutable. Traits can be appended, replaced, or decayed.                              |\n    81→| `Fragmented` | Temporarily split into derivative pieces for remixing / fractional DAO ownership.         |\n    82→| `Archived`   | Finalized. No further state transitions possible.                                         |\n    83→\n    84→### 3.2 ‑ Transition Rules\n    85→\n    86→1. `Freeze` requires multi-sig approval by **creator + ≥51 % current holders**.\n    87→2. `Inspire` requires PoI-selected composer **+ gRPC verification**.\n    88→3. `Evolve*` may only occur once every _epoch_ (`config.evolution_cadence`).\n    89→4. `Merge` requires `Fragmented` tokens to reference the same `origin_hash`.\n    90→5. `Burn` is irreversible and must pass the runtime `BurnPolicy` strategy.\n    91→\n    92→---\n    93→\n    94→## 4 ‑ gRPC API (`proto/nft_evolution.proto`)\n    95→\n    96→```proto\n    97→syntax = \"proto3\";\n    98→\n    99→package canvaschain.nft.v1;\n   100→\n   101→service Evolution {\n   102→  rpc Inspire(InspireRequest) returns (InspireResponse);\n   103→  rpc Freeze(FreezeRequest)   returns (FreezeResponse);\n   104→  rpc Evolve(EvolveRequest)   returns (EvolveResponse);\n   105→  rpc Split(SplitRequest)     returns (SplitResponse);\n   106→  rpc Merge(MergeRequest)     returns (MergeResponse);\n   107→  rpc Burn(BurnRequest)       returns (BurnResponse);\n   108→}\n   109→\n   110→message InspireRequest  { string nft_id = 1; bytes vrf_proof = 2; }\n   111→message InspireResponse { bool   accepted = 1; }\n   112→\n   113→message FreezeRequest   { string nft_id = 1; repeated string signatures = 2; }\n   114→message FreezeResponse  { bool   accepted = 1; }\n   115→\n   116→message EvolveRequest   { string nft_id = 1; bytes patch = 2; }\n   117→message EvolveResponse  { string new_cid = 1; }\n   118→\n   119→message SplitRequest    { string nft_id = 1; uint32 parts = 2; }\n   120→message SplitResponse   { repeated string child_ids = 1; }\n   121→\n   122→message MergeRequest    { repeated string child_ids = 1; }\n   123→message MergeResponse   { string merged_id = 1; }\n   124→\n   125→message BurnRequest     { string nft_id = 1; }\n   126→message BurnResponse    { bool burned = 1; }\n   127→```\n   128→\n   129→---\n   130→\n   131→## 5 ‑ Rust Reference Implementation (core excerpt)\n   132→\n   133→```rust\n   134→//! nft_runtime/src/state_machine.rs\n   135→use crate::error::NftError;\n   136→use chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\n   137→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n   138→\n   139→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]\n   140→pub enum Phase {\n   141→    Seeded,\n   142→    Dormant,\n   143→    Flourishing,\n   144→    Fragmented,\n   145→    Archived,\n   146→}\n   147→\n   148→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n   149→pub struct EvolutionEvent {\n   150→    pub nft_id: String,\n   151→    pub from: Phase,\n   152→    pub to: Phase,\n   153→    pub tx_hash: String,\n   154→    pub timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,\n   155→}\n   156→\n   157→impl EvolutionEvent {\n   158→    pub fn sanity_check(&self) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n   159→        if self.from == self.to {\n   160→            return Err(NftError::InvalidTransition(\n   161→                \"from and to phase are identical\".into(),\n   162→            ));\n   163→        }\n   164→        Ok(())\n   165→    }\n   166→}\n   167→\n   168→pub trait Evolvable {\n   169→    fn current_phase(&self) -> Phase;\n   170→    fn transition(&mut self, to: Phase) -> Result<EvolutionEvent, NftError>;\n   171→}\n   172→\n   173→impl Evolvable for Nft {\n   174→    fn current_phase(&self) -> Phase {\n   175→        self.phase.clone()\n   176→    }\n   177→\n   178→    fn transition(&mut self, to: Phase) -> Result<EvolutionEvent, NftError> {\n   179→        ensure_transition_is_valid(self.phase.clone(), to.clone())?;\n   180→        let event = EvolutionEvent {\n   181→            nft_id: self.id.clone(),\n   182→            from: self.phase.clone(),\n   183→            to: to.clone(),\n   184→            tx_hash: blake3::hash(self.id.as_bytes()).to_string(),\n   185→            timestamp: Utc::now(),\n   186→        };\n   187→        event.sanity_check()?;\n   188→        self.phase = to;\n   189→        Ok(event)\n   190→    }\n   191→}\n   192→\n   193→fn ensure_transition_is_valid(from: Phase, to: Phase) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n   194→    use Phase::*;\n   195→    match (from, to) {\n   196→        (Seeded, Dormant)\n   197→        | (Dormant, Flourishing)\n   198→        | (Flourishing, Flourishing)\n   199→        | (Flourishing, Fragmented)\n   200→        | (Fragmented, Dormant)\n   201→        | (Flourishing, Archived)\n   202→        | (Dormant, Archived) => Ok(()),\n   203→\n   204→        _ => Err(NftError::InvalidTransition(format!(\n   205→            \"illegal transition {:?} → {:?}\",\n   206→            from, to\n   207→        ))),\n   208→    }\n   209→}\n   210→```\n   211→\n   212→_Error definitions omitted; see `error.rs`._\n   213→\n   214→---\n   215→\n   216→## 6 ‑ Event-Driven Flow\n   217→\n   218→1. *Client* signs and submits `EvolveRequest`.\n   219→2. **NFT Runtime Svc** validates patch and calls `Nft::transition(Flourishing)`.\n   220→3. Upon success it publishes:\n   221→\n   222→```json\n   223→{\n   224→  \"topic\": \"canvaschain.nft.evolved\",\n   225→  \"payload\": {\n   226→    \"nft_id\": \"0xA11CE\",\n   227→    \"new_cid\": \"bafybeia...\",\n   228→    \"epoch\": 1192\n   229→  }\n   230→}\n   231→```\n   232→\n   233→4. **Observer UI** receives the event via WebSocket bridge and performs a live\n   234→   transition animation, fetching the new asset layer from IPFS.\n   235→\n   236→---\n   237→\n   238→## 7 ‑ Strategy-Based Burn Policy (pluggable)\n   239→\n   240→```rust\n   241→pub trait BurnPolicy: Send + Sync {\n   242→    fn can_burn(&self, nft: &Nft, requester: &Address) -> bool;\n   243→}\n   244→\n   245→pub struct SimpleBurn;\n   246→impl BurnPolicy for SimpleBurn {\n   247→    fn can_burn(&self, nft: &Nft, requester: &Address) -> bool {\n   248→        nft.creator == *requester\n   249→    }\n   250→}\n   251→\n   252→pub struct GovernanceBurn {\n   253→    pub quorum: u8,\n   254→}\n   255→impl BurnPolicy for GovernanceBurn {\n   256→    fn can_burn(&self, nft: &Nft, _requester: &Address) -> bool {\n   257→        dao::current_support(nft.id()) >= self.quorum\n   258→    }\n   259→}\n   260→```\n   261→\n   262→The active strategy is selected at runtime through a *chain spec* parameter,\n   263→enabling experimental collectives to upgrade without a hard-fork.\n   264→\n   265→---\n   266→\n   267→## 8 ‑ Edge Cases & Error Handling\n   268→\n   269→| Case                         | Response                                   |\n   270→|------------------------------|--------------------------------------------|\n   271→| Transition during lock       | `LOCKED` gRPC status.                     |\n   272→| Epoch cadence violation      | `FAILED_PRECONDITION` with retry-after.    |\n   273→| Missing VRF proof on Inspire | `UNAUTHENTICATED`.                         |\n   274→| Merge with mismatched DNA    | `INVALID_ARGUMENT`.                        |\n   275→\n   276→---\n   277→\n   278→## 9 ‑ Migration Notes (v0.9 → v1.0)\n   279→\n   280→1. `Paused` state removed; use `Dormant`.\n   281→2. `Evolve` cadence changed to epoch-based (was block-based).\n   282→3. `Split` now returns deterministic `child_ids` derived from BLAKE3 tree.\n   283→\n   284→---\n   285→\n   286→## 10 ‑ Open Questions\n   287→\n   288→1.
